        Engineering brief produced from the codebase and task details:

        ### Task Summary
        Produce a rigorous design document for extracting the Remotion video editor (reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/) into a standalone, OSS-worthy multi-package repository (schema + engine + editor + CLI) and wiring it back into reigh-app via a reference custom DataProvider adapter. The document is doc-mode only — no code changes — and must specify package layout, ports-and-adapters contracts, timeline JSON schema/versioning, OSS repo structure, OSS polish checklist, and a concrete reigh-app migration plan (files move/stay/split, host-touchpoint mapping, order of operations).

        ### Key Evidence
        - Task mandates a four-package split with specific responsibilities: versioned TimelineConfig JSON schema (zod + JSON Schema export + migration fn), pure Remotion engine (TimelineConfig, frame, assetResolver) -> pixels with zero UI/host coupling, React editor package using ports-and-adapters (DataProvider, AssetResolver, MediaPicker, Exporter, HostContext) each with in-memory defaults, and a CLI renderer `npx <name> render timeline.json out.mp4`.         ### Constraints
        - Doc-mode only: produce a design document, no code changes, no file moves, no partial implementations.
- Extracted packages must contain ZERO reigh-specific concepts — document must name the forbidden list explicitly (shot groups, generation assets, AgentChat, task-based export, Supabase, projectId, MediaLightbox) and route each to a port or app-level layer.
- Audience is mixed: (a) OSS contributors evaluating whether to adopt/build on the extracted packages, (b) reigh-app engineers executing the migration. Sections must serve both — keep OSS-facing and reigh-facing concerns in distinct, labeled parts.
- Each port must ship an in-memory default so the editor runs standalone with zero host wiring. Document must state what each default does/doesn't provide.
- Timeline JSON schema versioning and migration mechanics are first-class — forward-compat rules (unknown-field preservation) and app-specific extension strategy must be concrete, not aspirational.
- OSS polish checklist must call out Remotion's company-license terms explicitly — this is a legal/adoption risk for downstream consumers, not just a footnote.
- Tone: engineering design doc, not marketing. Favor tables, interface signatures, and numbered steps over prose. No emoji.
- Comprehensive but navigable — include a table of contents at the top; keep each section scannable with clear subheadings.
- Every claim about current reigh-app code must be traceable to a file path. The design step should open the files listed in `relevant_code` to verify current shapes before writing specifics (brief phase was sandbox-blocked from reading file contents).
- Document must include a concrete file-by-file (or folder-by-folder) migration table for every subdirectory under video-editor/ — not just high-level guidance.

        ### Suggested Approach
        Structure the design document in three parts plus an appendix.

1. Executive summary (~0.5 page) — problem, thesis, non-goals. Table of contents.

2. Part A — The extracted OSS repo (audience: OSS contributors):
   a. Package layout (schema / engine / editor / cli) — table with responsibility, public exports, peer deps, out-of-scope.
   b. Rationale per split (why engine is pure, why editor is ports-and-adapters, why CLI is separate).
   c. Port interfaces — one subsection each for DataProvider, AssetResolver, MediaPicker, Exporter, HostContext. For each: TS signature, error semantics, optional flags, in-memory default description.
   d. Timeline JSON format spec — version field, zod shape (summarized), JSON Schema export, migration function contract, core-vs-app-extension namespace rule, forward-compat rules.
   e. Effect registry placement decision (engine vs editor) with rationale.
   f. OSS repo structure — pnpm workspaces + Turborepo tree, examples/, apps/playground, apps/docs, CI pipeline.
   g. OSS polish checklist — license (Remotion terms flagged), README pitch outline, playground scope, docs site, changesets/release hygiene, TS strictness, ESM-first + CJS fallback, sourcemaps, `exports` map example.
   h. CLI renderer spec — invocation, flags, exit codes, default AssetResolver behavior.
   i. Test strategy — what moves where, what's new (port contract tests).

3. Part B — Reigh-app integration (audience: reigh engineers):
   a. Forbidden-in-core list with one-line reason per item.
   b. File-by-file (or folder-by-folder) migration table for video-editor/ — columns: path, decision (MOVE/STAY/SPLIT), destination, notes. Enumerate every subdirectory: compositions, components, effects, hooks, lib, contexts, settings, types, plus top-level `registration.test.ts`.
   c. Host-touchpoint -> port/app-layer mapping table — touchpoint, current location, new home (port name or app extension), what the extracted package exposes to support it.
   d. Reference Supabase DataProvider adapter — sketch of how `hooks/useTimelineQueries.ts` + `hooks/useTimelineRealtime.ts` become the reigh-side adapter implementing the DataProvider port.
   e. AgentChat / task-system / shot-groups as app-level extensions — pattern: app subscribes to editor's public store and renders overlays/panels; document the store API surface needed.
   f. Post-change import shapes — before/after examples for 3-4 representative files.
   g. Order of operations (numbered: extract schema in-place, then engine, then editor with local relative imports, flip to workspace:* linking, validate, publish v0.1, flip reigh to npm). Include rollback plan.
   h. Sync strategy during transition — option analysis (monorepo-then-split vs submodule vs local link vs dual-commit) with a recommendation.

4. Part C — Open questions and risks (audience: both):
   a. Remotion company-license implications for downstream consumers — what to surface in README, what adopters need to check.
   b. Pre-1.0 schema stability commitment — proposal (additive-only within a major; breaking changes require a migration fn).
   c. Engine renderer abstraction — tradeoff: keep Remotion-specific now (simpler, leaky) vs abstract now (bigger surface, future optionality). Recommendation with criteria for revisiting.
   d. Other risks (dependency drift, effect registry ABI stability, test-coverage gaps post-move).

5. Appendix — glossary (TimelineConfig, port, adapter, extension, HostContext), references to files in reigh-app that informed decisions.

        Idea:
Design plan to extract the Remotion video editor (currently at reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/) into a standalone, OSS-worthy repository, then wire the extracted package back into reigh-app to replace the in-tree editor. This is a doc-mode run — produce a rigorous design document, not code. EXTRACTED REPO: Versioned hand-editable TimelineConfig JSON schema as the core contract, with zod validators, JSON Schema export, and a migration function interface; Pure Remotion engine package: (TimelineConfig, frame, assetResolver) -> pixels, no UI or host coupling — enables SSR / CLI / headless rendering; React editor package using ports-and-adapters for: DataProvider (persistence), AssetResolver, MediaPicker, Exporter, HostContext config; each port ships a minimal in-memory default so the editor runs standalone; CLI renderer: npx <name> render timeline.json out.mp4; Examples: local-JSON editor, custom-backend adapter, headless render; Deployable playground (Vercel); Pristine TS (no any), tree-shakeable ESM-first with CJS fallback, sourcemaps, narrow package.json#exports, changesets, tests on pure logic (schema migrations, store reducers, coord math). REIGH-APP INTEGRATION: Reigh's current Supabase persistence becomes the reference custom DataProvider adapter living in reigh-app; Reigh-specific concepts (shot groups, generation assets, AgentChat bridge, task-based export) must NOT live in extracted packages — they stay in reigh-app and consume the editor's public store/API; Concrete migration plan: which files move, which stay, which split; post-change import shapes; order of operations that minimizes breakage; How each host-specific touchpoint currently baked in (auth, projectId, MediaLightbox, AgentChat registration, task system) becomes an injected port or app-level layer. DESIGN DELIVERABLES: Final package layout (schema/engine/editor/cli) with rationale for each split; Each port interface signature + in-memory default; Timeline JSON format spec (versioning strategy, migration mechanics, app-specific vs core fields, forward-compat rules); Repo structure for the OSS repo (pnpm workspaces + Turborepo, examples/, apps/playground, docs site, CI); OSS polish checklist (license choice accounting for Remotion's licensing terms, README pitch, playground, docs site, release hygiene); Open questions and risks: Remotion license implications for downstream, schema stability commitment pre-1.0, how to keep extracted repo in sync with reigh-app during the transition, whether the engine boundary should abstract over renderers so Remotion could be swapped later.

        Requirements:
        - If the engineering brief suggests an approach, use it as your starting hypothesis — but before committing, consider if there's a simpler or more fundamental fix. The brief is well-researched input, not a final answer.
        - If the brief is absent, incomplete, or says "skip", inspect the repository yourself before planning.
        - Stay focused on the requested idea. If repo exploration surfaces unrelated issues or docs, ignore them and return to the task.
        - Prefer source code, tests, and directly relevant config files. Avoid `.megaplan/`, prior plan artifacts, and unrelated `docs/` or ops/deployment material unless the task explicitly depends on them.
        - Stop exploring once you have enough evidence to name the concrete touch points and validation path. Do not keep browsing after you can write the plan.
        - Produce a concrete implementation plan in markdown.
        - Define observable success criteria as objects with `criterion` (string) and `priority` (`must`, `should`, or `info`):
          - `must` — hard gate. The reviewer will block on failure. Use for correctness, functional requirements, and verifiable outcomes (e.g., "all existing tests pass", "API returns 200 for valid input"). Every `must` criterion must have a clear yes/no answer.
          - `should` — quality target. The reviewer flags but does not block. Use for subjective goals, numeric guidelines, and best-effort improvements (e.g., "file under ~300 lines", "no deeply nested conditionals", "each function has a single responsibility").
          - `info` — documented for humans, reviewer skips. Use for criteria that cannot be verified in this pipeline (e.g., "13 manual smoke tests pass", "stakeholder sign-off obtained").
        - Each success criterion should include a `requires` field listing the capabilities needed for verification. Valid capability strings: `run_shell`, `read_files`, `run_tests`, `parse_diff`, `read_build_output`, `run_linter` (container), `drive_browser`, `inspect_runtime_ui`, `observe_runtime_logs`, `subjective_judgment`, `verify_physical_device` (human). `must` criteria MUST have non-empty `requires`. Example: `{"criterion": "All tests pass", "priority": "must", "requires": ["run_tests"]}`.
        - Use the `questions` field for ambiguities that would materially change implementation.
        - Use the `assumptions` field for defaults you are making so planning can proceed now.
        - Prefer cheap validation steps early.
        - Keep the plan proportional to the task. A 1-line fix needs a 2-step plan (apply fix + run tests), not a 5-step investigation.
        - If user notes answer earlier questions, incorporate them into the draft plan instead of re-asking them.
        - Fix the problem fully. Do not limit scope just to avoid breaking existing tests — update the tests too if needed.
        - Prefer the simplest, most direct fix. No fallbacks, type conversions, or defensive wrappers without concrete evidence they are needed.
        - If the task or issue hints suggest a specific approach, follow it. Only deviate with concrete counter-evidence.

        Plan template — simple format (adapt to the actual repo and scope):
````md
# Implementation Plan: [Title]

## Overview
Summarize the goal, current repository shape, and the constraints that matter.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Audit the current behavior (`megaplan/prompts.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Inspect** the current implementation and call out the exact insertion points (`megaplan/prompts.py:29`).

### Step 2: Add the first change (`megaplan/evaluation.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** the smallest viable change with exact file references (`megaplan/evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Capture** any tricky behavior with a short example.
   ```python
   issues = validate_plan_structure(plan_text)
   ```

### Step 3: Wire downstream behavior (`megaplan/handlers.py`, `megaplan/workers.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Update** the runtime flow in the touched files (`megaplan/handlers.py:400`, `megaplan/workers.py:199`).

### Step 4: Prove the change (`tests/test_evaluation.py`, `tests/test_megaplan.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** the cheapest targeted checks first (`tests/test_evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Finish** with broader verification once the wiring is in place (`tests/test_megaplan.py:1`).

## Execution Order
1. Update prompts and mocks before enforcing stricter validation.
2. Land higher-risk wiring after the validator and tests are ready.

## Validation Order
1. Start with focused unit tests.
2. Run the broader suite after the flow changes are in place.
````

For complex plans, use multiple phases:
````md

### Step 1: Install dependencies (`package.json`)
...

### Step 2: Create database migration (`supabase/migrations/`)
...

## Phase 2: Core Integration

````

Template guidance:
- Simple plans: use `## Main Phase` with `### Step N:` sections underneath.
- Complex plans: use multiple `## Phase N:` sections, each containing `### Step N:` steps. Step numbers are global (not per-phase).
- The flat `## Step N:` format (without phases) also works for backwards compatibility.
- Key invariants: one H1 title, one `## Overview`, numbered step sections (`### Step N:` or `## Step N:`), and at least one ordering section.

        ### Test Expectations
        - [pass_to_pass] Executive summary & goals: Under one page: why extract, who benefits (OSS audience, reigh-app), what reigh-app gains, explicit non-goals (e.g., not a full NLE replacement).
- [pass_to_pass] Package layout & boundary rationale: Names, responsibilities, and dependency arrows for schema / engine / editor / cli. For each: public exports, peer deps, what is explicitly out of scope. Rationale for every split — not just a diagram.
- [pass_to_pass] Port interfaces (DataProvider, AssetResolver, MediaPicker, Exporter, HostContext): Each port shown as a TypeScript interface signature with parameter/return types, error semantics, optional-method flags. Each port has a stated in-memory default with notes on what it does/doesn't support (e.g., in-memory DataProvider loses data on reload).
- [pass_to_pass] Timeline JSON format specification: Concrete v1 schema (zod shape summarized), version field, migration function contract `(prev: unknown, fromVersion: number) => TimelineConfig`, separation of core fields vs app-specific extension namespace (e.g., `app: Record<string, unknown>` or `x-reigh-*` convention), forward-compat rules (unknown fields preserved through round-trips).
- [pass_to_pass] OSS repo structure: pnpm workspaces + Turborepo layout shown as a tree: packages/schema, packages/engine, packages/editor, packages/cli, examples/local-json, examples/custom-backend, examples/headless-render, apps/playground, apps/docs. One-line rationale per entry. CI outline (lint, typecheck, test, build, changesets release).
- [pass_to_pass] Reigh-app migration plan (files move/stay/split): Concrete table classifying every subdirectory under video-editor/ as MOVE (to which extracted package), STAY (in reigh-app as host glue), or SPLIT (what goes where). Post-change import paths for reigh-app. Specific files called out: DataProviderContext, TimelineRenderer, AgentChat/, ShotGroup*, Lightbox*, useActiveTaskClips, useTimelineRealtime.
- [pass_to_pass] Host-touchpoint -> port/app-layer mapping: For each coupling point (Supabase persistence, auth, projectId, MediaLightbox, AgentChat, task-based export, shot groups, generation assets): which port absorbs it OR which app-level extension hosts it, and what the extracted package exposes to enable that.
- [pass_to_pass] Order of operations minimizing breakage: Numbered migration sequence: extract schema first (in-place), then engine, then editor with reigh still consuming via relative imports, flip to `workspace:*`/`file:` linking, validate, publish v0.1, flip reigh to npm. Includes a staging strategy and rollback plan.
- [pass_to_pass] CLI renderer spec: `npx <name> render timeline.json out.mp4` surface: flags (fps, resolution, codec, concurrency, asset-root), exit codes, how asset resolution works in a CLI context (local filesystem default AssetResolver).
- [pass_to_pass] Examples & playground: Three examples with one-line purposes (local-JSON editor, custom-backend adapter, headless render) and Vercel-deployable playground brief (what it demos, where sample timelines come from).
- [pass_to_pass] OSS polish checklist: License choice with Remotion licensing implications called out explicitly (not just 'MIT'), README pitch structure, docs site outline, changesets workflow, release hygiene, pristine TS rules (no `any`), tree-shakeable ESM-first with CJS fallback, sourcemaps, narrow `package.json#exports` example.
- [pass_to_pass] Test strategy: Which existing tests survive and move where (schema migrations in schema package, coord math + reducers in engine/editor), which become integration tests in reigh-app (Supabase-backed DataProvider adapter), which are new (port contract tests using in-memory defaults).
- [pass_to_pass] Open questions and risks: Explicit list covering: (a) Remotion company-license implications for downstream users, (b) pre-1.0 schema stability commitment and deprecation policy, (c) how to keep extracted repo in sync with reigh-app during transition (monorepo-then-split vs submodule vs local link vs dual-commit), (d) engine-boundary renderer abstraction so Remotion could be swapped (tradeoffs: leaky abstraction vs future optionality).

        ### Constraints
        - Doc-mode only: produce a design document, no code changes, no file moves, no partial implementations.
- Extracted packages must contain ZERO reigh-specific concepts — document must name the forbidden list explicitly (shot groups, generation assets, AgentChat, task-based export, Supabase, projectId, MediaLightbox) and route each to a port or app-level layer.
- Audience is mixed: (a) OSS contributors evaluating whether to adopt/build on the extracted packages, (b) reigh-app engineers executing the migration. Sections must serve both — keep OSS-facing and reigh-facing concerns in distinct, labeled parts.
- Each port must ship an in-memory default so the editor runs standalone with zero host wiring. Document must state what each default does/doesn't provide.
- Timeline JSON schema versioning and migration mechanics are first-class — forward-compat rules (unknown-field preservation) and app-specific extension strategy must be concrete, not aspirational.
- OSS polish checklist must call out Remotion's company-license terms explicitly — this is a legal/adoption risk for downstream consumers, not just a footnote.
- Tone: engineering design doc, not marketing. Favor tables, interface signatures, and numbered steps over prose. No emoji.
- Comprehensive but navigable — include a table of contents at the top; keep each section scannable with clear subheadings.
- Every claim about current reigh-app code must be traceable to a file path. The design step should open the files listed in `relevant_code` to verify current shapes before writing specifics (brief phase was sandbox-blocked from reading file contents).
- Document must include a concrete file-by-file (or folder-by-folder) migration table for every subdirectory under video-editor/ — not just high-level guidance.

        ### Suggested Approach
        Structure the design document in three parts plus an appendix.

1. Executive summary (~0.5 page) — problem, thesis, non-goals. Table of contents.

2. Part A — The extracted OSS repo (audience: OSS contributors):
   a. Package layout (schema / engine / editor / cli) — table with responsibility, public exports, peer deps, out-of-scope.
   b. Rationale per split (why engine is pure, why editor is ports-and-adapters, why CLI is separate).
   c. Port interfaces — one subsection each for DataProvider, AssetResolver, MediaPicker, Exporter, HostContext. For each: TS signature, error semantics, optional flags, in-memory default description.
   d. Timeline JSON format spec — version field, zod shape (summarized), JSON Schema export, migration function contract, core-vs-app-extension namespace rule, forward-compat rules.
   e. Effect registry placement decision (engine vs editor) with rationale.
   f. OSS repo structure — pnpm workspaces + Turborepo tree, examples/, apps/playground, apps/docs, CI pipeline.
   g. OSS polish checklist — license (Remotion terms flagged), README pitch outline, playground scope, docs site, changesets/release hygiene, TS strictness, ESM-first + CJS fallback, sourcemaps, `exports` map example.
   h. CLI renderer spec — invocation, flags, exit codes, default AssetResolver behavior.
   i. Test strategy — what moves where, what's new (port contract tests).

3. Part B — Reigh-app integration (audience: reigh engineers):
   a. Forbidden-in-core list with one-line reason per item.
   b. File-by-file (or folder-by-folder) migration table for video-editor/ — columns: path, decision (MOVE/STAY/SPLIT), destination, notes. Enumerate every subdirectory: compositions, components, effects, hooks, lib, contexts, settings, types, plus top-level `registration.test.ts`.
   c. Host-touchpoint -> port/app-layer mapping table — touchpoint, current location, new home (port name or app extension), what the extracted package exposes to support it.
   d. Reference Supabase DataProvider adapter — sketch of how `hooks/useTimelineQueries.ts` + `hooks/useTimelineRealtime.ts` become the reigh-side adapter implementing the DataProvider port.
   e. AgentChat / task-system / shot-groups as app-level extensions — pattern: app subscribes to editor's public store and renders overlays/panels; document the store API surface needed.
   f. Post-change import shapes — before/after examples for 3-4 representative files.
   g. Order of operations (numbered: extract schema in-place, then engine, then editor with local relative imports, flip to workspace:* linking, validate, publish v0.1, flip reigh to npm). Include rollback plan.
   h. Sync strategy during transition — option analysis (monorepo-then-split vs submodule vs local link vs dual-commit) with a recommendation.

4. Part C — Open questions and risks (audience: both):
   a. Remotion company-license implications for downstream consumers — what to surface in README, what adopters need to check.
   b. Pre-1.0 schema stability commitment — proposal (additive-only within a major; breaking changes require a migration fn).
   c. Engine renderer abstraction — tradeoff: keep Remotion-specific now (simpler, leaky) vs abstract now (bigger surface, future optionality). Recommendation with criteria for revisiting.
   d. Other risks (dependency drift, effect registry ABI stability, test-coverage gaps post-move).

5. Appendix — glossary (TimelineConfig, port, adapter, extension, HostContext), references to files in reigh-app that informed decisions.

IMPORTANT for the design step: begin by opening every file listed in `relevant_code` to verify current shapes (sandbox restrictions blocked reading in the brief phase, so only filenames/structure are confirmed). In particular, verify the current DataProviderContext interface, the TimelineConfig/Clip/Track type definitions, and the TimelineRenderer host-coupling surface before finalizing port signatures and the migration table.
        The engineering brief above was produced by analyzing the codebase. Use it as a strong starting point, but the suggested approach is a hypothesis — verify it's the best fix, not just a valid one.

        Idea:
Design plan to extract the Remotion video editor (currently at reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/) into a standalone, OSS-worthy repository, then wire the extracted package back into reigh-app to replace the in-tree editor. This is a doc-mode run — produce a rigorous design document, not code. EXTRACTED REPO: Versioned hand-editable TimelineConfig JSON schema as the core contract, with zod validators, JSON Schema export, and a migration function interface; Pure Remotion engine package: (TimelineConfig, frame, assetResolver) -> pixels, no UI or host coupling — enables SSR / CLI / headless rendering; React editor package using ports-and-adapters for: DataProvider (persistence), AssetResolver, MediaPicker, Exporter, HostContext config; each port ships a minimal in-memory default so the editor runs standalone; CLI renderer: npx <name> render timeline.json out.mp4; Examples: local-JSON editor, custom-backend adapter, headless render; Deployable playground (Vercel); Pristine TS (no any), tree-shakeable ESM-first with CJS fallback, sourcemaps, narrow package.json#exports, changesets, tests on pure logic (schema migrations, store reducers, coord math). REIGH-APP INTEGRATION: Reigh's current Supabase persistence becomes the reference custom DataProvider adapter living in reigh-app; Reigh-specific concepts (shot groups, generation assets, AgentChat bridge, task-based export) must NOT live in extracted packages — they stay in reigh-app and consume the editor's public store/API; Concrete migration plan: which files move, which stay, which split; post-change import shapes; order of operations that minimizes breakage; How each host-specific touchpoint currently baked in (auth, projectId, MediaLightbox, AgentChat registration, task system) becomes an injected port or app-level layer. DESIGN DELIVERABLES: Final package layout (schema/engine/editor/cli) with rationale for each split; Each port interface signature + in-memory default; Timeline JSON format spec (versioning strategy, migration mechanics, app-specific vs core fields, forward-compat rules); Repo structure for the OSS repo (pnpm workspaces + Turborepo, examples/, apps/playground, docs site, CI); OSS polish checklist (license choice accounting for Remotion's licensing terms, README pitch, playground, docs site, release hygiene); Open questions and risks: Remotion license implications for downstream, schema stability commitment pre-1.0, how to keep extracted repo in sync with reigh-app during the transition, whether the engine boundary should abstract over renderers so Remotion could be swapped later.

        Current plan (markdown):
        # Implementation Plan: Design Doc — Extract Remotion Video Editor to OSS Repo

## Overview
Produce a single rigorous design document (markdown) specifying how to extract `reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/` into a standalone multi-package OSS repo (`schema` / `engine` / `editor` / `cli`) and re-integrate it into reigh-app via a reference custom DataProvider adapter.

**Mode:** Doc-only. No code changes, no file moves, no stubs. Output is a markdown document in the reigh-workspace root (path decided in Step 1).

**Audience:** Dual — OSS contributors evaluating adoption, and reigh-app engineers executing the migration. Sections must be labeled for the right audience.

**Anchoring constraint:** The brief's fourteen `pass_to_pass` sections (exec summary, package layout & rationale, 5 port interfaces, JSON schema spec, repo structure, migration plan, host-touchpoint map, order of operations, CLI spec, examples/playground, OSS polish, test strategy, open questions) are the acceptance checklist — the doc's table of contents must cover all of them.

**Repo shape (known):** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/` with subdirs `compositions/`, `components/` (TimelineEditor, PreviewPanel, AgentChat, VideoEditorLightboxOverlay, …), `hooks/`, `lib/`, `contexts/` (DataProviderContext), `effects/` (DynamicEffectRegistry), `settings/`, `types/`, plus `registration.test.ts` at root. Every subdir must appear in the file-by-file migration table.

**Forbidden-in-core list (must be named explicitly in doc):** shot groups, generation assets, AgentChat, task-based export, Supabase, auth, projectId, MediaLightbox.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Confirm document location and verify current file shapes (`reigh-workspace/`, `reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/**`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Confirm** the output path for the design doc with the user. Default assumption: `reigh-workspace/docs/video-editor-extraction-design.md` (new file). Alternative: top-level `reigh-workspace/VIDEO_EDITOR_EXTRACTION.md`. This lives in reigh-workspace, not reigh-app, because the doc spans both the new OSS repo and reigh-app.
2. **Open and read** every file listed in the brief's `relevant_code` table before writing specifics. The brief's planning phase was sandbox-blocked from reading these; the design phase must not repeat that mistake. Mandatory reads:
   - `reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/contexts/DataProviderContext.tsx` — verify current interface methods, signatures, return types. Drives the DataProvider port spec.
   - `reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/types/index.ts` + `types/timeline-canvas.ts` + `types/agent-session.ts` — derive v1 TimelineConfig schema baseline and separate core vs host-specific fields.
   - `reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/compositions/TimelineRenderer.tsx` + `VisualClip.tsx` + `AudioTrack.tsx` + `EffectLayerSequence.tsx` — confirm zero host coupling or enumerate exactly what must be lifted out to reach "pure engine".
   - `reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineEditor.tsx` + `TimelineCanvas.tsx` — determine public `<Editor />` prop surface (ports + HostContext).
   - `reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/effects/DynamicEffectRegistry.ts` (+ test) — confirm registration API so placement decision (engine vs editor) is grounded.
   - `reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useTimelineQueries.ts` + `useTimelineRealtime.ts` — derive DataProvider port methods including optional `subscribe(timelineId)` semantics.
   - `reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useActiveTaskClips.ts` + `useAgentSession.ts` + `components/AgentChat/index.ts` + `components/VideoEditorLightboxOverlay.tsx` + `components/TimelineEditor/ShotGroupContextMenu.tsx` + `ShotGroupOverlay.tsx` — confirm these are the canonical host-coupling examples; derive the public store API surface the editor must expose to support them as app-level extensions.
   - `reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/settings/videoEditorDefaults.ts` + `useEditorSettings.ts` + `lib/defaults.ts` — seed for in-memory port defaults and HostContext surface.
   - `reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/lib/config-utils.ts` — core of the schema package's validation/migration logic.
   - `reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/registration.test.ts` — preserve the host tool-registration seam in the post-migration story.
   - `reigh-app/package.json` — pin peerDependency ranges for Remotion, zod, zustand, React Query so extracted packages align.
3. **Inventory** every subdirectory under `video-editor/` with `ls`/`Glob` so no folder is omitted from the migration table.
4. **Stop exploring** once the above are read. Do not browse unrelated parts of the repo.

### Step 2: Draft the executive summary and TOC (`docs/video-editor-extraction-design.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Write** a ≤ 1 page executive summary: problem (video editor is buried in reigh-app, reusable pieces trapped), thesis (four-package split behind ports), who benefits (OSS audience gets a reusable Remotion editor; reigh-app gets a cleaner seam and OSS leverage), explicit non-goals (not a full NLE replacement, not abandoning reigh-app's current features, no renderer swap in v1).
2. **Emit** a numbered table of contents covering all fourteen `pass_to_pass` sections, organized into Part A (OSS repo), Part B (reigh-app integration), Part C (open questions & risks), Appendix.

### Step 3: Part A — Package layout, port interfaces, schema spec (`docs/video-editor-extraction-design.md`)
**Scope:** Large
1. **Package layout table** — columns: package name, responsibility, public exports, peer deps, out-of-scope. Four rows: `@<scope>/schema`, `@<scope>/engine`, `@<scope>/editor`, `@<scope>/cli`. One-paragraph rationale per split (why engine is pure, why editor is ports-and-adapters, why CLI is a separate package not a script).
2. **Port interface subsections** — one per port. For each: TypeScript interface signature, parameter/return types, error semantics (typed errors vs thrown), optional-method flags, concrete in-memory default description, notes on what the default does/doesn't provide.
   - `DataProvider` — load/save/list/subscribe(?)/delete; derived from `DataProviderContext.tsx` and `useTimelineQueries.ts`/`useTimelineRealtime.ts`. In-memory default: localStorage or pure-memory map, loses data on reload if memory-only.
   - `AssetResolver` — `resolve(assetRef) => { url, mime, duration? }`; default resolves `file://` + `http(s)://` URIs directly.
   - `MediaPicker` — `pick(options) => Promise<Asset | null>`; default opens a native `<input type="file">`.
   - `Exporter` — `render(config, options) => Promise<Blob | URL>`; default uses Remotion Player's local render or delegates to the CLI.
   - `HostContext` — feature flags, brand colors, locale, theme; default ships sensible values from `settings/videoEditorDefaults.ts`.
   - For each port: state the **gap** between the current reigh implementation (e.g., DataProviderContext shape) and the target OSS port — what methods must be added, renamed, or removed before extraction.
3. **Timeline JSON format spec** — `version: 1`, zod shape summary (tracks, clips, effects, transitions), JSON Schema export via `zod-to-json-schema`, migration function contract `(prev: unknown, fromVersion: number) => TimelineConfig`, separation of core fields vs app-specific extensions (recommendation: single `app: Record<string, unknown>` field with a `x-<namespace>` convention inside, so reigh stores shot groups/generation assets under `app["x-reigh"]`), forward-compat rules (unknown fields preserved through round-trips; migrations are additive-only within a major).
4. **Effect registry placement** — decision (recommend: registry lives in `engine` with a thin editor-side registration helper, so headless CLI rendering supports the same effects), rationale, registration API signature.
5. **OSS repo structure** — pnpm workspaces + Turborepo tree:
   ```
   packages/{schema,engine,editor,cli}
   examples/{local-json,custom-backend,headless-render}
   apps/{playground,docs}
   ```
   One-line rationale per entry. CI outline: lint, typecheck, test, build, changesets release, sourcemap upload.
6. **OSS polish checklist** —
   - License: recommend MIT/Apache-2.0 for the extracted packages but **explicitly** document Remotion's commercial-license terms (free for individuals and small companies; paid license required above a team-size threshold) and the adopter's obligation to verify compliance. Put a `REMOTION_LICENSE.md` at repo root.
   - README pitch structure (one-sentence hook, problem/solution, 60-second demo, install, minimal example).
   - Playground scope (Vercel-deployable; edits a sample timeline; no backend).
   - Docs site outline (Nextra or similar; sections: getting started, ports, schema, CLI, examples).
   - Changesets workflow, release hygiene (no manual version bumps).
   - Pristine TS: `strict: true`, no `any`, no `@ts-ignore` without linked issue.
   - ESM-first + CJS fallback, sourcemaps, narrow `package.json#exports` example.
7. **CLI renderer spec** — `npx <name> render <timeline.json> <out.mp4>`; flags: `--fps`, `--width`, `--height`, `--codec`, `--concurrency`, `--asset-root`, `--quality`; exit codes (0 success, 1 validation error, 2 render error, 3 IO error); default AssetResolver (local filesystem relative to `--asset-root`, fallback to URL).
8. **Test strategy** — schema migration tests live in `packages/schema`; coord math (`render-bounds`, `timeline-scale`) and reducers (`resolve-overlaps`, `duplicate-clip`) in `packages/engine` or `packages/editor` per split; port contract tests (new) using the in-memory defaults; reigh-side integration tests for the Supabase DataProvider adapter. Map every existing test file listed in the brief to its new home.

### Step 4: Part B — Reigh-app migration plan (`docs/video-editor-extraction-design.md`)
**Scope:** Large
1. **Forbidden-in-core list** — explicit bulleted list of concepts that must not appear in extracted packages: shot groups, generation assets, AgentChat, task-based export, Supabase, auth, projectId, MediaLightbox. One-line reason per item.
2. **Folder-by-folder migration table** — one row per subdirectory plus relevant top-level files. Columns: `path`, `decision` (MOVE / STAY / SPLIT), `destination`, `notes`. Mandatory coverage:
   - `compositions/` → MOVE to `packages/engine`
   - `components/TimelineEditor/` → SPLIT: core canvas/toolbar MOVE to `packages/editor`; `ShotGroupContextMenu.tsx` + `ShotGroupOverlay.tsx` STAY in reigh-app as app-level extensions
   - `components/PreviewPanel/RemotionPreview.tsx` → MOVE to `packages/editor`
   - `components/AgentChat/` → STAY
   - `components/VideoEditorLightboxOverlay.tsx` → STAY (reigh-side adapter for `MediaPicker` port)
   - `contexts/DataProviderContext.tsx` → SPLIT: port interface to `packages/editor`; in-memory default to same package; reigh-specific Supabase adapter STAY in reigh-app
   - `effects/` → MOVE to `packages/engine` (with registration API surfaced)
   - `hooks/` → SPLIT per-hook: pure-logic hooks (`__tests__/resolve-overlaps`) MOVE; Supabase/task/agent hooks STAY
   - `lib/` → SPLIT: coord math + config utils MOVE; anything host-specific STAYs
   - `settings/` → SPLIT: defaults MOVE as port defaults; reigh-app settings STAY
   - `types/` → SPLIT: core timeline types MOVE to `packages/schema`; `agent-session.ts` STAYs
   - `registration.test.ts` → STAY (adapted to register the extracted editor)
3. **Host-touchpoint → port/app-layer mapping table** — columns: touchpoint, current location, new home (port name or app extension), what the extracted package exposes. Rows for: Supabase persistence, auth, projectId, MediaLightbox, AgentChat, task-based export, shot groups, generation assets.
4. **Reference Supabase DataProvider adapter** — sketch how `useTimelineQueries.ts` + `useTimelineRealtime.ts` become the reigh-side adapter. Show the adapter file layout inside reigh-app (e.g., `reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor-host/DataProvider.supabase.ts`) and its `subscribe()` implementation using Supabase realtime channels.
5. **App-level extensions pattern** — AgentChat, task system, shot groups subscribe to the editor's public store (Zustand selector export) and render overlays via the editor's extension-slot props. Document the minimum public store API surface needed (selectors + actions) and the `renderOverlay` / `panels` slot props on `<TimelineEditor />`.
6. **Post-change import shapes** — before/after examples for 3–4 representative files (e.g., the main tool entry, a shot-group consumer, the Supabase adapter).
7. **Order of operations** — numbered, breakage-minimizing sequence:
   1. Extract `schema` package in place (move `types/` + `lib/config-utils.ts`) — reigh still imports via relative paths.
   2. Extract `engine` (move `compositions/` + `effects/` + pure math).
   3. Extract `editor` with reigh still consuming via relative imports / tsconfig paths.
   4. Flip reigh to `workspace:*` or `file:` link, validate end-to-end.
   5. Publish v0.1 of each package.
   6. Flip reigh to npm versions.
   Include a rollback plan at each step (revert the import-path flip; keep old tree until v0.1 is stable).
8. **Sync strategy during transition** — option analysis table (monorepo-then-split via `git subtree split`, git submodule, local `file:` link, dual-commit). Recommendation with rationale.

### Step 5: Part C — Open questions and risks (`docs/video-editor-extraction-design.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Remotion company-license implications** — surface the threshold, what README must warn, what adopters must verify, contact link. Not a footnote.
2. **Pre-1.0 schema stability commitment** — proposal: additive-only within a major; breaking changes ship with a `migrate(fromVersion → toVersion)` function and a deprecation window.
3. **Engine renderer abstraction** — tradeoff analysis (keep Remotion-specific now = simpler, leaky; abstract now = bigger surface, future optionality). Recommendation: keep Remotion-specific in v1 with a documented "extract renderer interface if we adopt a second renderer" trigger.
4. **Keeping repo in sync during transition** — linked to Step 4.8 choice.
5. **Other risks** — Remotion peer-dependency drift, effect registry ABI stability, test-coverage gaps after moves, examples becoming stale.

### Step 6: Appendix + final review (`docs/video-editor-extraction-design.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Glossary** — TimelineConfig, port, adapter, extension, HostContext, AssetRef, MigrationFn.
2. **File references** — list of reigh-app files that informed the design, each with a one-line reason.
3. **Self-review checklist against the brief's fourteen `pass_to_pass` sections** — confirm each is present, scannable, and meets its bullet-level requirements. Tone: engineering design, no emoji, tables over prose where feasible.

## Execution Order
1. Confirm doc path (user) and read the `relevant_code` files first — nothing else is safe to write until port shapes and types are verified.
2. Draft exec summary + TOC to lock the skeleton.
3. Fill Part A (OSS-facing) top to bottom.
4. Fill Part B (reigh-facing) — the folder-by-folder table is the gating artifact.
5. Fill Part C + appendix.
6. Self-review against the brief's checklist.

## Validation Order
1. Self-check each `pass_to_pass` item is present and concrete (not aspirational).
2. Verify every current-code claim in the doc is traceable to a file path read in Step 1.
3. Verify the forbidden-in-core list is named and each item is routed to a port or app layer.
4. Verify the folder-by-folder migration table covers every subdirectory under `video-editor/`.
5. Verify Remotion licensing is called out explicitly, not as a footnote.

  "signals": {
    "iteration": 1,
    "idea": "Design plan to extract the Remotion video editor (currently at reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/) into a standalone, OSS-worthy repository, then wire the extracted package back into reigh-app to replace the in-tree editor. This is a doc-mode run \u2014 produce a rigorous design document, not code. EXTRACTED REPO: Versioned hand-editable TimelineConfig JSON schema as the core contract, with zod validators, JSON Schema export, and a migration function interface; Pure Remotion engine package: (TimelineConfig, frame, assetResolver) -> pixels, no UI or host coupling \u2014 enables SSR / CLI / headless rendering; React editor package using ports-and-adapters for: DataProvider (persistence), AssetResolver, MediaPicker, Exporter, HostContext config; each port ships a minimal in-memory default so the editor runs standalone; CLI renderer: npx <name> render timeline.json out.mp4; Examples: local-JSON editor, custom-backend adapter, headless render; Deployable playground (Vercel); Pristine TS (no any), tree-shakeable ESM-first with CJS fallback, sourcemaps, narrow package.json#exports, changesets, tests on pure logic (schema migrations, store reducers, coord math). REIGH-APP INTEGRATION: Reigh's current Supabase persistence becomes the reference custom DataProvider adapter living in reigh-app; Reigh-specific concepts (shot groups, generation assets, AgentChat bridge, task-based export) must NOT live in extracted packages \u2014 they stay in reigh-app and consume the editor's public store/API; Concrete migration plan: which files move, which stay, which split; post-change import shapes; order of operations that minimizes breakage; How each host-specific touchpoint currently baked in (auth, projectId, MediaLightbox, AgentChat registration, task system) becomes an injected port or app-level layer. DESIGN DELIVERABLES: Final package layout (schema/engine/editor/cli) with rationale for each split; Each port interface signature + in-memory default; Timeline JSON format spec (versioning strategy, migration mechanics, app-specific vs core fields, forward-compat rules); Repo structure for the OSS repo (pnpm workspaces + Turborepo, examples/, apps/playground, docs site, CI); OSS polish checklist (license choice accounting for Remotion's licensing terms, README pitch, playground, docs site, release hygiene); Open questions and risks: Remotion license implications for downstream, schema stability commitment pre-1.0, how to keep extracted repo in sync with reigh-app during the transition, whether the engine boundary should abstract over renderers so Remotion could be swapped later.",

## Overview
Summarize the goal, current repository shape, and the constraints that matter.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Audit the current behavior (`megaplan/prompts.py`)
**Scope:** Small

### Step 2: Add the first change (`megaplan/evaluation.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

   ```python

### Step 3: Wire downstream behavior (`megaplan/handlers.py`, `megaplan/workers.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

### Step 4: Prove the change (`tests/test_evaluation.py`, `tests/test_megaplan.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** the cheapest targeted checks first (`tests/test_evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Finish** with broader verification once the wiring is in place (`tests/test_megaplan.py:1`).

## Execution Order
1. Update prompts and mocks before enforcing stricter validation.
2. Land higher-risk wiring after the validator and tests are ready.

## Validation Order
1. Start with focused unit tests.
2. Run the broader suite after the flow changes are in place.
````

For complex plans, use multiple phases:

...

Template guidance:
- Simple plans: use `## Main Phase` with `### Step N:` sections underneath.
- Complex plans: use multiple `## Phase N:` sections, each containing `### Step N:` steps. Step numbers are global (not per-phase).
- The flat `## Step N:` format (without phases) also works for backwards compatibility.
- Key invariants: one H1 title, one `## Overview`, numbered step sections (`### Step N:` or `## Step N:`), and at least one ordering section.

In-memory default: pure-memory map; does not persist across reloads; no-ops optional methods.\n   - **`AssetResolver`** — derived from current `resolveAssetUrl` callers in `lib/timeline-data.ts`, `hooks/useTimelinePersistence.ts`, `hooks/usePollSync.ts`, `lib/external-drop-utils.ts`. Signature: `resolve(assetRef) => { url, mime, duration? }`. Default resolves `file://` + `http(s)://` URIs directly.\n   - **`MediaPicker`** — anchored on `components/VideoEditorLightboxOverlay.tsx`. Default opens a native `<input type=\"file\">`.\n   - **`Exporter`** — anchored on `hooks/useClientRender.ts`, `hooks/useRenderState.ts`, `hooks/useTimelineState.ts`, `components/VideoEditorShell.tsx`. Default uses Remotion Player's local render; the CLI package is a parallel Exporter implementation.\n   - **`HostContext`** — derived from `settings/videoEditorDefaults.ts`, `settings/useEditorSettings.ts`. Carries feature flags, brand colors, locale, theme. Default ships sensible values.\n3. **Timeline JSON format spec — must reconcile with existing code (FLAG-003, correctness-1, correctness-2):**\n   - **Migration away from `pinnedShotGroups`:** The current `TimelineConfig` in `types/index.ts` contains `pinnedShotGroups`, which is reigh-specific and forbidden-in-core. The v1 OSS schema **removes** this field from the core shape; a required migration function relocates it into `app[\"x-reigh\"].pinnedShotGroups` during the first read. Document the migration fn contract: `migrate(prev: unknown, fromVersion: number) => TimelineConfig`.\n   - **Serializer reconciliation:** `lib/serialize.ts` today rejects unknown top-level, clip, and track keys. State the concrete change required: (option A, recommended) whitelist a single `app: Record<string, unknown>` key at top-level, clip, and track levels — additive and backwards-compatible; (option B) loosen the serializer to preserve all unknown keys. Pick option A explicitly.\n   - **Zod shape summary** — tracks, clips, effects, transitions, `app?: Record<string, unknown>` at each level.\n   - **Version field** — `version: 1`.\n   - **JSON Schema export** via `zod-to-json-schema`.\n   - **Forward-compat rules** — unknown keys inside `app` preserved through round-trips; unknown keys outside `app` still rejected (maintains current safety). Migrations are additive-only within a major.\n4. **Effect registry placement** — decision (recommend: registry lives in `engine` so headless CLI rendering supports the same effects), rationale, registration API signature. Cite `effects/DynamicEffectRegistry.ts:`.\n5. **OSS repo structure** — pnpm workspaces + Turborepo tree:\n   ```\n   packages/{schema,engine,editor,cli}\n   examples/{local-json,custom-backend,headless-render}\n   apps/{playground,docs}\n   ```\n   One-line rationale per entry. CI outline: lint, typecheck, test, build, changesets release, sourcemap upload.\n6. **OSS polish checklist** —\n   - **License**: recommend MIT for the extracted packages with a dedicated `REMOTION_LICENSE.md` at repo root explaining Remotion's commercial-license terms and what adopters must verify (team size, commercial use).\n   - README pitch structure (hook, problem/solution, 60-second demo, install, minimal example).\n   - Playground scope (Vercel-deployable; edits a sample timeline; no backend).\n   - Docs site outline (Nextra or similar; sections: getting started, ports, schema, CLI, examples).\n   - Changesets workflow, release hygiene.\n   - Pristine TS: `strict: true`, no `any`, no `@ts-ignore` without linked issue.\n   - ESM-first + CJS fallback, sourcemaps, narrow `package.json#exports` example.\n7. **CLI renderer spec** — `npx <name> render <timeline.json> <out.mp4>`; flags: `--fps`, `--width`, `--height`, `--codec`, `--concurrency`, `--asset-root`, `--quality`; exit codes (0 success, 1 validation error, 2 render error, 3 IO error); default AssetResolver (local filesystem relative to `--asset-root`, fallback to URL).\n8. **Test strategy** — map every existing test to a destination package: schema migrations → `packages/schema`; coord math (`render-bounds`, `timeline-scale`) and reducers (`resolve-overlaps`, `duplicate-clip`) → `packages/engine`/`packages/editor` per split; port contract tests (new) using in-memory defaults; reigh-side integration tests for the Supabase DataProvider adapter. Name every existing `*.test.ts` under the editor tree.\n\n### Step 4: Part B — Reigh-app migration plan (`docs/video-editor-extraction-design.md`)\n**Scope:** Large\n1. **Forbidden-in-core list** — explicit bulleted list: shot groups (incl. `pinnedShotGroups` — flag as migrated-out), generation assets, AgentChat, task-based export, Supabase, auth, projectId, MediaLightbox, project selection, shots context, `VideoGenerationModal`, generation duplication, final-video availability, stale-variant handling, active-task tracking.         - [pass_to_pass] Executive summary & goals: Under one page: why extract, who benefits (OSS audience, reigh-app), what reigh-app gains, explicit non-goals (e.g., not a full NLE replacement).
